  "abstract_verbatim": "Using the companion critical Bessel-height and pin-limit theorems, we prove that at the mass-defined critical inverse temperature of the nearest-neighbor XY model on the square lattice, the spin field on a square with boundary angles fixed to zero converges along the full sequence to the full-variance imaginary exponential of a zero-Dirichlet Gaussian free field with stiffness $`2/\\pi`$. <a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> Using the companion critical Bessel-height and pin-limit theorems, we prove that at the mass-defined critical inverse temperature of the nearest-neighbor XY model on the square lattice, the spin field on a square with boundary angles fixed to zero converges along the full sequence to the full-variance imaginary exponential of a zero-Dirichlet Gaussian free field with stiffness $`2/\pi`$. The normalization uses the exact center magnetization and the lattice Green-function factor. Convergence holds in law in $`H^{-3}_{\mathrm{loc}}((-1,1)^2)`$; all mixed moments and joint laws of fields smeared against smooth compactly supported test functions in $`(-1,1)^2`$ converge as well.

- **claim-critical-spin-field-limit** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): For the fixed-boundary critical XY law <a href="#eq:pinned-spin-law" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:pinned-spin-law">[eq:pinned-spin-law]</a>, the distributions $S_n$ in <a href="#eq:spin-field" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:spin-field">[eq:spin-field]</a> converge in law along the full sequence $n\to\infty$ to $V_*$ in $H^{-3}_{\mathrm{loc}}(D)$. Moreover, for every $k\ge1$, every $f_1,\ldots,f_k\in C_c^\infty(D;\mathbb C)$, and every collection of nonnegative integers $p_1,q_1,\ldots,p_k,q_k$, $$\label{eq:main-moment-convergence} \mathbb E_n^0\prod_{j=1}^k S_n(f_j)^{p_j}\overline{S_n(f_j)}^{\,q_j} \longrightarrow \mathbb E\prod_{j=1}^k V_*(f_j)^{p_j}\overline{V_*(f_j)}^{\,q_j}.$$ The joint laws of these complex smeared fields converge as well.
  - Explanation: The exact center magnetization and diagonal Green correction identify the critical spin field with full-variance imaginary Gaussian chaos, including nonneutral mixed moments and joint smeared laws.
- **claim-fixed-boundary-height-duality** (theorem, [source](#stmt-heights-duality)): Let $\mathcal G_n$ have as vertices the centers of the lattice squares in $[-1,1]^2$, and join centers of neighboring squares. This is the free dual square grid. Given integers $l_x$, indexed by the interior primal vertices, choose an integral connection $s$ on $\mathcal G_n$ whose circulation around $x$ is $2\pi l_x$, with a consistent choice of orientation. Then $$\mathbb E_n^0\exp\left(i\sum_xl_x\theta_x\right)=F_{\mathcal G_n}^s.$$ Such a connection exists without a neutrality assumption. If holes containing the charged vertices are removed, its restriction to the remaining graph is flat, with periods specified by the enclosed charges. On that graph the bare partition ratio depends only on those periods.
  - Explanation: Fixed boundary spins permit charges to flow to the boundary, so the dual height representation works without neutrality. Removing charged regions leaves a flat connection determined by its periods.
- **claim-critical-height-coefficient** (theorem, [source](#stmt-heights-critical-input)): For the free-box spin mass threshold $b_c$ of Section <a href="#sec:intro" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:intro">1</a>, the Bessel height coefficient satisfies $a(b_c)=8\pi$.
  - Explanation: The critical Bessel height coefficient is imported from the companion critical-height result with the same mass threshold and normalization.
- **claim-free-rectangle-gaussian-limits** (theorem, [source](#stmt-heights-free-input)): Let $U_n$ be free axis-parallel rectangles at mesh $n^{-1}$, with endpoints converging to those of a nondegenerate rectangle $U$. For any finite collection of smooth functions defined near $\overline U$, the observations $\langle h,A_{U_n}g\rangle$ converge jointly, in Laplace transforms and all moments, to centered Gaussians with covariance $$a\int_U\nabla g\cdot\nabla g'.$$ For bounded piecewise continuous mean-zero densities and the exactly centered profiles just specified, height smears converge jointly in Laplace transforms and all moments, with covariance $a(-\Delta_{U,N})^{-1}$, where the inverse is on mean-zero functions.
  - Explanation: Free rectangular height observations converge jointly in Laplace transforms and moments. Mean-free smears have the continuum Neumann covariance.
- **claim-mixed-geometry-height-limits** (theorem, [source](#stmt-heights-mixed-input)): In any fixed mixed geometry, the centered massless Bessel height law with primary hard pins has Gaussian Laplace and moment limits for each finite collection of bounded piecewise continuous density smears. On a component $U$ meeting a pin $p$, the covariance is the inverse of the form $$a^{-1}\int_U\|\nabla u\|^2 \quad\hbox{on}\quad \{u\in H^1(U):u=0\text{ almost everywhere on }p\}.$$ Free walls have the variational Neumann condition. On an unpinned component, the assertion concerns mean-free smears; the fractional mean modulo $2\pi$ tends to an independent uniform variable.* *A fixed finite-rank nonnegative quadratic penalty in box averages gives the corresponding Gaussian limit. When the penalty controls every unpinned component constant, lattice translation copies are summed and the limiting constants are integrated. The assertions hold along every admissible sequence of rounded discretizations.
  - Explanation: Fixed mixed geometries admit Gaussian limits with hard pins, free walls, and finite-rank quadratic observations. Released component constants require mean-free tests or explicit confinement.
- **claim-separated-primary-pin-determinant** (theorem, [source](#stmt-heights-pin-input)): In a fixed mixed geometry, start with no hard pins and add the centered penalty $\\|Bh\\|^2/2$, where $B$ is a fixed finite list of component-local box averages controlling every component constant. For positively separated pin regions $H,p$, put $$D_n(H,p)= \log\frac{\mathbb P(h_H=0\mid h_p=0)}{\mathbb P(h_H=0)}.$$ Then $D_n(H,p)\ge0$ and it converges to the Gaussian pin interaction for the Hilbert-space inner product associated with $$\mathcal T(u)=a^{-1}\sum_U\int_U\|\nabla u\|^2+\\|Bu\\|^2, \qquad u\in\bigoplus_U H^1(U).$$ More precisely, with orthogonality and projections taken for $\mathcal T$, $$\begin{gathered} V_Q=\{u:u=0\text{ almost everywhere on }Q\}^{\perp_{\mathcal T}}, \qquad \Pi_Q=\operatorname{proj}_{V_Q},\qquad C=\Pi_H\|_{V_p},\\ D_n(H,p)\longrightarrow -\frac12\log\det(1-C^*C). \end{gathered} \tag{1}\label{eq:source-1}$$ The operator $C$ is Hilbert–Schmidt and has norm strictly below one.
  - Explanation: The interaction of separated absolute pin events converges to a Gaussian projection determinant. The confining observations control every component constant.
- **claim-finite-height-precision-comparisons** (theorem, [source](#stmt-heights-finite-comparisons)): Allow nonnegative quadratic observation penalties and compatible linear equality constraints on a finite primary height graph. The following comparisons hold.* 1. *Increasing centered precision decreases centered covariance. Consequently, in a proper centered law, the probability of an additional centered linear equality increases under a centered precision increase. The same assertion holds in a quotient law for equalities invariant under its released constants.* 2. *A shifted-to-centered partition ratio is at most one and decreases when the same nonnegative precision is added in the affine and centered coordinates. Shifts may include integral connections, nonzero prescribed data, and arbitrary real observation centers. The monotonicity persists with a fixed real linear statistic in the numerator exponent; the bound by one is not asserted for that tilted ratio.* 3. *Equality constraints are obtained by increasing their quadratic penalties to infinity. Free constants can be released when all data and real tests in question are invariant under those constants.
  - Explanation: Centered precision decreases covariance and shifted partition ratios. The untilted ratio is bounded by its centered counterpart; real numerator tilts retain monotonicity but need not retain that upper bound.
- **claim-cut-height-comparison** (theorem, [source](#stmt-heights-cut-comparison)): Let $G^{\rm cut}$ be obtained from $G$ by deleting edges, with the same observation penalties and compatible prescribed data on both graphs. Each retained observation and each relative-data set is component-local in $G^{\rm cut}$. Whenever the free constants are left free by those data, $$\frac{Z_G^s(u)}{Z_G^0(0_p)} \le \frac{Z_{G^{\rm cut}}^s(u)} {Z_{G^{\rm cut}}^0(0_p)}.$$ The conclusion also holds with absolute data fixing some constants and with observations confining others. It holds for a fixed real numerator tilt on affine edge increments if each deleted increment is retained independently, with its tilt, on the cut side. Every real test on a released component must annihilate its constant.
  - Explanation: Deleting edges increases the relevant affine-to-centered ratio when observations and prescribed data respect cut components. Real tests on released constants must vanish on those constants.
- **claim-restoration-zero-data-bound** (bound, [source](#stmt-heights-restoration)): For an edge restoration with prescribed absolute or relative data on $p$, define $$T^s(u)=\frac{Z_{\rm restored}^s(u)}{Z_{\rm cut}^s(u)}, \qquad d=T^0(0_p).$$ Use quotient constants on free components and sum all relative integer translations when components are joined. Then $$0<T^s(u)\le d.$$
  - Explanation: A restoration factor at compatible prescribed data is positive and bounded by its centered zero-data value, with relative integer translations summed when pieces join.
- **claim-fixed-hole-sector-energy** (theorem, [source](#stmt-sectors-positive-radius)): For the domain and periods just specified, at $b=b_c$ and $a=8\pi$, $$\lim_{n\to\infty}F_{U,n}^s =\exp\{-\mathcal M(U)/(2a)\}, \qquad \mathcal M(U)=\inf_{g\in H^1(U)} \int_U\|dv_0+dg\|^2 . \tag{3}\label{eq:source-3}$$ The limit holds along every admissible mesh-rounding sequence. The variational energy depends only on the periods.
  - Explanation: On a fixed punctured polygonal domain, the magnetic-sector ratio converges to the exponential of the minimum energy with its prescribed periods.
- **claim-cell-confinement-localization** (theorem, [source](#stmt-sectors-localization)): For every fixed dyadic mixed geometry and fixed dyadic pin regions $H,p$ as above, $$\lim_{d\downarrow0}\limsup_{n\to\infty}D_{n,d}(H,p)=0 . \tag{4}\label{eq:source-4}$$ Here $d$ runs through dyadic values aligned with every fixed geometric division. The mesh limit is taken at each fixed $d$.
  - Explanation: Increasing the density of cell-average observations suppresses the interaction between separated pins. The mesh limit precedes refinement of the observation cells.
- **claim-single-lift-confined-energy** (theorem, [source](#stmt-sectors-single-lift)): Let $A$ be a component of a fixed dyadic mixed geometry, with free boundary, and suppose it admits a single-valued smooth lift $v_A$ of $v$. For its confined ratio, the mesh limit exists at each fixed $d$, and $$\lim_{d\downarrow0}\lim_{n\to\infty} R_{A,n,d}^s(v) =\exp\left\{-\frac1{2a}\int_A\|dv\|^2\right\}.$$ The same assertion holds for finitely many such pieces, with free constants treated independently.
  - Explanation: A smooth single-valued lift turns the confined sector ratio into a Gaussian transform. Refining the observation cells recovers the lift's Dirichlet energy.
- **claim-multivalued-confined-sector-limit** (theorem, [source](#stmt-sectors-weighted)): For the observations and smooth multivalued $v$ just defined, $$\lim_{d\downarrow0}\limsup_{n\to\infty} \left\|R_{n,d}^s(v)- \exp\left\{-\frac1{2a}\int_U\|dv\|^2\right\}\right\|=0. \tag{5}\label{eq:source-5}$$ The observation partitions may be required to align with any fixed finite collection of the cuts, patches, and pin bands constructed below.
  - Explanation: Slitting the domain produces pieces with single-valued lifts. Separated-pin localization makes the restoration losses vanish, preserving the total energy.
- **claim-long-annulus-flat-pin-decoupling** (theorem, [source](#stmt-annuli-flat-interaction)): Fix $P$ as above. For either choice of $H$, the interaction $\overline D(H,p)$ is nonnegative and $$\limsup_{\mathrm{mesh}\to0}\overline D(H,p) \quad\text{can be made arbitrarily small by taking \(\lambda\) large.} \tag{7}\label{eq:source-7}$$ The mesh limit is taken at each fixed polygonal geometry.
  - Explanation: The interaction of flat pin events at a guard and an annular end is nonnegative and becomes small on long annuli after taking the mesh limit at fixed geometry.
- **claim-interface-kernel-bounds** (bound, [source](#stmt-kernels-bounds)): For each of the kernels just defined, every sector and every compatible guard datum satisfy $$0<T_i^s/d_i\le1. \tag{8}\label{eq:source-8}$$ For an interface between two annuli, choose the end region $H$ on each side containing its seam endpoints. For the joint exterior kernel choose the outer end region in each incident annulus. Then $$1\ge \int (T_i^0/d_i) \prod_{\text{incident annuli }\ell}dm_\ell^0 \ge \prod_{\text{incident annuli }\ell} e^{-\overline D_\ell(H,p)} . \tag{9}\label{eq:source-9}$$
  - Explanation: Normalized restoration kernels have a pointwise upper bound, while their centered averages have lower bounds controlled by separated flat-pin interactions.
- **claim-annular-exact-factorization** (theorem, [source](#stmt-kernels-multiplication)): Let a core be surrounded by $N$ annular blocks, with no other adjacencies between the blocks. Their restored partition function is $$\label{kernels:product} Z_{\mathrm{full}}^s = Z_{\mathrm{core}}^s\prod_{i=1}^N Z_i^s \int T_0^s(u_1) \prod_{i=1}^{N-1}T_i^s(u_i,u_{i+1}) \prod_{i=1}^N dm_i^s(u_i).$$ For several disjoint nests attached to a common connected exterior, the corresponding formula includes its cut partition function and the one joint exterior kernel.
  - Explanation: The partition function factors into cut-block partition functions and an integral of interface kernels. A common exterior contributes a joint kernel.
- **claim-regular-interface-small-average-loss** (bound, [source](#stmt-kernels-average-loss)): For a regular interface, $$\int(T_i^s/d_i)\prod_\ell dm_\ell^s = \left(\int(T_i^0/d_i)\prod_\ell dm_\ell^0\right) \frac{F_{\mathrm{restored}}^s}{F_{\mathrm{cut}}^s}. \tag{10}\label{eq:source-10}$$ Given any $\xi>0$, first choose $\varepsilon$ sufficiently small and fix a polygon $P$ satisfying its disk inclusions. Then choose $\lambda$ sufficiently large. There is a fixed aligned threshold $R_0$ such that every regular interface with smaller inner scale at least $R_0$ satisfies $$\int(1-T_i^s/d_i)\prod_\ell dm_\ell^s\le\xi \tag{11}\label{eq:source-11}$$ in each of the two unit-period sectors and in the centered sector. The individual flat-pin interactions in Equation <a href="#eq:source-9" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:source-9">[eq:source-9]</a> can also be made arbitrarily small with these choices.
  - Explanation: The restoration average is related exactly to a sector-ratio comparison. Suitable fixed annular geometry makes the average loss small in centered and unit-period sectors.
- **claim-positive-kernel-terminal-stability** (theorem, [source](#stmt-kernels-stability)): Let $(X_i,m_i)$, $i\ge1$, be probability spaces, and let $t_i:X_i\times X_{i+1}\to(0,1]$ be measurable kernels with $$\int(1-t_i)\,d(m_i\otimes m_{i+1})\le\xi,\qquad 0<\xi<1/64.$$ Fix a measurable $g:X_1\to(0,1]$, positive $m_1$-almost everywhere. For each $N$, give $X_1\times\cdots\times X_N$ density proportional to $$g(u_1)\prod_{i=1}^{N-1}t_i(u_i,u_{i+1})$$ with respect to $\prod_i m_i$. For all sufficiently large $N$, its last-coordinate marginal has density at most $2$ with respect to $m_N$. No positive essential lower bound on $g$ is required.
  - Explanation: Small average kernel losses contract centered density remainders. A fixed positive initial weight eventually yields a bounded terminal density without a positive essential lower bound.
- **claim-annular-last-guard-domination** (bound, [source](#stmt-kernels-last-guard)): For these fixed choices, the last-guard law in a nest of $N$ annuli has density at most $2$ relative to $m_N^s$ for all sufficiently large $N$, in the centered sector and in either unit-period sector. The conclusion holds for identical lattice translates of the nest with the same threshold in $N$.
  - Explanation: The abstract stability result controls the last guard in centered and unit-period annular nests, uniformly under identical lattice translations.
- **claim-punctured-domain-energy-expansion** (theorem, [source](#stmt-correlations-exterior-energy)): Remove the closed disks of radius $\rho$ about the distinct points $z_1,\ldots,z_k$ from $D$, where $\rho>0$ is small enough that these disks are disjoint and contained in $D$. For affine gradients with periods $2\pi\sigma_i$ about the holes, the minimum energy of Equation <a href="#eq:source-3" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:source-3">[eq:source-3]</a> satisfies $$\mathcal M =2\pi k\log(1/\rho) +4\pi^2H_D(\mathbf z,\boldsymbol\sigma)+O(\rho). \tag{12}\label{eq:source-12}$$ The error constant may depend on the separated interior configuration, but is uniform for all sufficiently small $\rho$.
  - Explanation: The exterior variational energy separates a universal logarithmic self-energy from the Green and Robin interactions of the marked points.
- **claim-moving-lattice-hole-bracketing** (bound, [source](#stmt-correlations-exterior-lattice)): Suppose that $\rho_n\to\rho>0$, where $\rho$ is sufficiently small. Around $x_{i,n}$, cut out the interiors in the array division with hole shape $\rho_nP$, using the rounding conventions of Section <a href="#sec:sectors" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:sectors">3</a>. Let $F_{\rm ext}^s$ be the sector ratio in the remaining dual graph, with periods $2\pi\sigma_i$ about these holes. Then $$\limsup_n \left\| \log F_{\rm ext}^s+ \frac{2\pi k\log(1/\rho)+4\pi^2H_D(\mathbf z,\boldsymbol\sigma)} {2a} \right\| \leq C(\varepsilon+\rho). \tag{13}\label{eq:source-13}$$ The constant $C$ depends on the separated limiting configuration but not on sufficiently small $\rho$ or $\varepsilon$. The same assertion holds after bounded-step mesh rounding.
  - Explanation: Fixed polygonal exteriors bracket moving lattice holes. The exterior sector ratio is controlled by the continuum energy with errors from hole shape and radius.
- **claim-small-exterior-attachment-loss** (bound, [source](#stmt-correlations-attachment)): For every sufficiently small $\xi>0$, the parameters may be chosen so that Equation <a href="#eq:source-11" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:source-11">[eq:source-11]</a> holds throughout the regular annuli and $$\limsup_n\int \left(1-\frac{T_{\rm ext}^s}{d_{\rm ext}}\right) \prod_{i=1}^k dm_{N,i}^s \leq\xi . \tag{14}\label{eq:source-14}$$ This holds both for the shifted and centered calculations and for the single center nest. The parameters depend on the fixed separated configuration and on $\xi$, but not on $n$. The errors in Equation <a href="#eq:source-13" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:source-13">[eq:source-13]</a> at the radii in this construction may simultaneously be made at most $\xi$.
  - Explanation: The joint exterior attachment has small average loss after the annular parameters are fixed suitably. The construction also controls the exterior energy error.
- **claim-center-normalized-separated-correlations** (theorem, [source](#stmt-correlations-separated)): For the separated moving marks fixed at the beginning of this section, $$\lim_n\log\left( \frac{\mathbb E_n^0\exp(i\sum_i\sigma_i\theta_{x_{i,n}})}{a_n^k} \right) = -\frac{H_D(\mathbf z,\boldsymbol\sigma)-kR_D(0,0)} {2K_*}. \tag{15}\label{eq:source-15}$$
  - Explanation: Identical microscopic nest factors cancel against the exact center magnetization, leaving the continuum Green and Robin interaction.
- **claim-green-diagonal-difference-limit** (theorem, [source](#stmt-correlations-green-diagonal)): If $x_n\in D_n^\circ$ and $x_n\to z\in D$, then $$G_n(x_n,x_n)-G_n(0,0) \longrightarrow R_D(z,z)-R_D(0,0). \tag{16}\label{eq:source-16}$$ These differences are bounded uniformly when $x_n$ ranges over a fixed compact subset of $D$.
  - Explanation: Subtracting diagonal Green values removes their common lattice divergence. The remaining difference converges to the corresponding Robin difference and stays bounded on interior compact sets.
- **claim-green-normalized-correlation-kernel** (theorem, [source](#stmt-correlations-normalized)): For every fixed separated moving configuration as above, $$\lim_n\mathbb E_n^0\prod_{i=1}^kW_n^{\sigma_i}(x_{i,n}) =\exp\left\{-\frac1{K_*} \sum_{i<j}\sigma_i\sigma_jG_D(z_i,z_j)\right\}. \tag{17}\label{eq:source-17}$$ The convergence to the same kernel evaluated at the lattice locations is uniform when those locations range over a compact interior set and their mutual distances are bounded below by a fixed positive number.
  - Explanation: The deterministic diagonal correction cancels the Robin self-terms, giving the signed Gaussian exponential kernel uniformly on compact separated configurations.
- **claim-nest-ratio-lower-bounds** (bound, [source](#stmt-correlations-nest-bounds)): There are constants $c,c'>0$, independent of $n,N,j$, such that, for all sufficiently large $n$, $$a_n\geq c\,F_{\rm nest}(R_{N(n)}), \tag{18}\label{eq:source-18}$$ and, for every $N\geq j\geq0$, $$F_{\rm nest}(R_N) \geq c'\lambda^{-(N-j)/2}F_{\rm nest}(R_j). \tag{19}\label{eq:source-19}$$ Here $j=0$ means the filled core alone.
  - Explanation: The center magnetization controls a full nest ratio from below, and nest ratios decay no faster than a fixed geometric lower bound across scales.
- **claim-two-point-collision-control** (bound, [source](#stmt-correlations-diagonal)): For every compact $E\Subset D$, there is $C_E<\infty$ such that, for all $n\geq1$, all $x,y\in E\cap D_n^\circ$, including $x=y$, and all $\sigma,\tau\in\{-1,1\}$, $$0\leq\mathbb E_n^0\bigl[W_n^\sigma(x)W_n^\tau(y)\bigr] \leq C_E\bigl(\|x-y\|+1/n\bigr)^{-1}. \tag{20}\label{eq:source-20}$$ Expectations of products of any number of the point fields $W_n^\sigma$, with arbitrary signs and repeated locations, are also nonnegative.
  - Explanation: An integrable two-point estimate remains valid at repeated locations and for either sign. Arbitrary signed products also have nonnegative expectation.
- **claim-lee-yang-absolute-moments** (bound, [source](#stmt-fields-lee-yang)): Consider a finite ferromagnetic XY model with nonnegative edge couplings and all boundary angles fixed to zero. For deterministic weights $\lambda_x\geq0$, set $$W=\sum_x\lambda_x e^{i\theta_x},\qquad v=\mathbb E\|W\|^2.$$ There is an absolute constant $C$ such that $$\label{fields:ly-bound} \\|W\\|_{L^p}\leq C\sqrt p\,v^{1/2},\qquad p\geq2.$$ For every finite list of vertices, including repetitions, and every choice of signs $\sigma_i\in\{-1,1\}$, one also has $$\label{fields:positive-moments} \mathbb E\exp\left\{i\sum_i\sigma_i\theta_{x_i}\right\}\geq0.$$
  - Explanation: The finite ferromagnetic model admits absolute moment bounds for arbitrary nonnegative spin weights, together with positivity for signed products including repeated vertices.
- **claim-circle-chaos-existence-and-moments** (construction, [source](#stmt-fields-circle-chaos)): Let $K>1/(4\pi)$ and let $\Phi_D$ be the zero-Dirichlet Gaussian free field with covariance $G_D=(-\Delta_D)^{-1}$. Where the circle of radius $\varepsilon$ about $x$ lies in $D$, define $$V_\varepsilon(x)= \exp\left\{\frac{i\Phi_{D,\varepsilon}(x)}{\sqrt K} +\frac{\mathbb E\Phi_{D,\varepsilon}(x)^2}{2K}\right\}.$$ There exists a complex random distribution $V_{K,D}\in H^{-2}_{\mathrm{loc}}(D)$ such that, for every $f\in C_c^\infty(D;\mathbb C)$, $V_\varepsilon(f)\to V_{K,D}(f)$ in every finite $L^p$. For every smooth compactly supported cutoff $\chi$, $\chi V_\varepsilon\to\chi V_{K,D}$ in $L^2(\Omega;H^{-2})$. For $m\geq1$, arbitrary signs $\sigma_i\in\{-1,1\}$, and $f_i\in C_c^\infty(D;\mathbb C)$, $$\label{fields:continuum-moments} \mathbb E\prod_{i=1}^m V_{K,D}^{\sigma_i}(f_i) =\int_{D^m}\prod_{i=1}^m f_i(x_i) \exp\left\{-\frac1K\sum_{i<j}\sigma_i\sigma_j G_D(x_i,x_j)\right\} \,dx_1\cdots dx_m.$$ The integral is absolutely convergent. In particular, $\mathbb EV_{K,D}(f)=\int_D f(x)\,dx$.
  - Explanation: Full-variance circle regularizations converge to a random distribution. Its mixed moments are absolutely convergent signed exponential Green-kernel integrals.
- **claim-uniform-smeared-field-bounds** (bound, [source](#stmt-fields-moment-bounds)): For every compact $E\Subset D$, every bounded complex function $f$ supported in $E$, and every $p\geq2$, $$\label{fields:test-bounds} \mathbb E_n^0\|S_n^\sigma(f)\|^2\leq C_E\\|f\\|_\infty^2, \qquad \\|S_n^\sigma(f)\\|_{L^p}\leq C_E\sqrt p\,\\|f\\|_\infty,$$ uniformly in $n$ and $\sigma\in\{-1,1\}$. If $B\subset E$ is a square of side $d\leq1$, then $$\label{fields:box-bounds} \mathbb E_n^0\|S_n^\sigma(1_B)\|^2\leq C_E(d+n^{-1})^3, \qquad \\|S_n^\sigma(1_B)\\|_{L^p} \leq C_E\sqrt p\,(d+n^{-1})^{3/2}.$$
  - Explanation: The collision estimate and Lee–Yang inequality bound smeared fields uniformly in the mesh. Small-square bounds make collision neighborhoods negligible.
- **claim-all-smeared-moments-converge** (theorem, [source](#stmt-fields-moments)): For every $m\geq1$, signs $\sigma_i\in\{-1,1\}$, and $f_i\in C_c^\infty(D;\mathbb C)$, $$\label{fields:lattice-moment-limit} \lim_{n\to\infty}\mathbb E_n^0\prod_{i=1}^m S_n^{\sigma_i}(f_i) =\mathbb E\prod_{i=1}^m V_*^{\sigma_i}(f_i).$$ The right side is the integral in Equation <a href="#fields:continuum-moments" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="fields:continuum-moments">[fields:continuum-moments]</a> with $K=K_*$.
  - Explanation: Separated correlation convergence and uniform removal of collision regions give every signed smeared moment of the continuum field.
- **claim-joint-smeared-laws-converge** (theorem, [source](#stmt-fields-law)): For every $k\geq1$ and $f_1,\ldots,f_k\in C_c^\infty(D;\mathbb C)$, $$(S_n(f_1),\ldots,S_n(f_k)) \ \Longrightarrow\ (V_*(f_1),\ldots,V_*(f_k)).$$ Every mixed moment of this vector and its complex conjugate converges.
  - Explanation: Uniform exponential integrability makes the limiting moments determine all real projections, identifying the joint complex field laws.
- **claim-local-sobolev-tightness-and-limit** (theorem, [source](#stmt-fields-tightness)): The laws of $S_n$ are tight in $H^{-3}_{\mathrm{loc}}(D)$. They converge there, along the full sequence, to the law of $V_*$.
  - Explanation: Localized Fourier moment bounds give compactness in the stated local Sobolev topology. The joint test-function laws identify every subsequential limit.

For any finite collection of smooth functions defined near $\\overline U$, the observations $\\langle h,A_{U_n}g\\rangle$ converge jointly, in Laplace transforms and all moments, to centered Gaussians with covariance $$a\\int_U\\nabla g\\cdot\\nabla g'.$$ For bounded piecewise continuous mean-zero densities and the exactly centered profiles just specified, height smears converge jointly in Laplace transforms and all moments, with covariance $a(-\\Delta_{U,N})^{-1}$, where the inverse is on mean-zero functions.",
          "source_id": "PM-216.3"
        }
      ],

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-heights-mixed-input",
        "exact_quote": "In any fixed mixed geometry, the centered massless Bessel height law with primary hard pins has Gaussian Laplace and moment limits for each finite collection of bounded piecewise continuous density smears. On a component $U$ meeting a pin $p$, the covariance is the inverse of the form $$a^{-1}\\int_U|\\nabla u|^2\n  \\quad\\hbox{on}\\quad\n  \\{u\\in H^1(U):u=0\\text{ almost everywhere on }p\\}.$$ Free walls have the variational Neumann condition. On an unpinned component, the assertion concerns mean-free smears; the fractional mean modulo $2\\pi$ tends to an independent uniform variable.*\n\n*A fixed finite-rank nonnegative quadratic penalty in box averages gives the corresponding Gaussian limit. When the penalty controls every unpinned component constant, lattice translation copies are summed and the limiting constants are integrated. The assertions hold along every admissible sequence of rounded discretizations.",
        "source_id": "PM-216.3"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-heights-mixed-input",
          "exact_quote": "In any fixed mixed geometry, the centered massless Bessel height law with primary hard pins has Gaussian Laplace and moment limits for each finite collection of bounded piecewise continuous density smears. On an unpinned component, the assertion concerns mean-free smears; the fractional mean modulo $2\\pi$ tends to an independent uniform variable.*\n\n*A fixed finite-rank nonnegative quadratic penalty in box averages gives the corresponding Gaussian limit.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-heights-pin-input",
        "exact_quote": "In a fixed mixed geometry, start with no hard pins and add the centered penalty $\\|Bh\\|^2/2$, where $B$ is a fixed finite list of component-local box averages controlling every component constant. For positively separated pin regions $H,p$, put $$D_n(H,p)=\n \\log\\frac{\\mathbb P(h_H=0\\mid h_p=0)}{\\mathbb P(h_H=0)}.$$ Then $D_n(H,p)\\ge0$ and it converges to the Gaussian pin interaction for the Hilbert-space inner product associated with $$\\mathcal T(u)=a^{-1}\\sum_U\\int_U|\\nabla u|^2+\\|Bu\\|^2,\n         \\qquad u\\in\\bigoplus_U H^1(U).$$ More precisely, with orthogonality and projections taken for $\\mathcal T$, $$\\begin{gathered}\n V_Q=\\{u:u=0\\text{ almost everywhere on }Q\\}^{\\perp_{\\mathcal T}},\n \\qquad \\Pi_Q=\\operatorname{proj}_{V_Q},\\qquad\n C=\\Pi_H|_{V_p},\\\\\n             D_n(H,p)\\longrightarrow\n                  -\\frac12\\log\\det(1-C^*C).\n \\end{gathered}\n \\tag{1}\\label{eq:source-1}$$ The operator $C$ is Hilbert–Schmidt and has norm strictly below one.",
        "source_id": "PM-216.3"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-heights-pin-input",
          "exact_quote": "In a fixed mixed geometry, start with no hard pins and add the centered penalty $\\|Bh\\|^2/2$, where $B$ is a fixed finite list of component-local box averages controlling every component constant.           "source_id": "PM-216.3"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "In a fixed mixed geometry, start with no hard pins and add the centered penalty $\\|Bh\\|^2/2$, where $B$ is a fixed finite list of component-local box averages controlling every component constant.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-heights-finite-comparisons",
        "exact_quote": "Allow nonnegative quadratic observation penalties and compatible linear equality constraints on a finite primary height graph. The following comparisons hold.*\n\n1.  *Increasing centered precision decreases centered covariance. Consequently, in a proper centered law, the probability of an additional centered linear equality increases under a centered precision increase. The same assertion holds in a quotient law for equalities invariant under its released constants.*\n\n2.  *A shifted-to-centered partition ratio is at most one and decreases when the same nonnegative precision is added in the affine and centered coordinates. Shifts may include integral connections, nonzero prescribed data, and arbitrary real observation centers. The monotonicity persists with a fixed real linear statistic in the numerator exponent; the bound by one is not asserted for that tilted ratio.*\n\n3.  *Equality constraints are obtained by increasing their quadratic penalties to infinity. Free constants can be released when all data and real tests in question are invariant under those constants.",
        "source_id": "PM-216.3"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-heights-finite-comparisons",
          "exact_quote": "Allow nonnegative quadratic observation penalties and compatible linear equality constraints on a finite primary height graph. The same assertion holds in a quotient law for equalities invariant under its released constants.*\n\n2.  *A shifted-to-centered partition ratio is at most one and decreases when the same nonnegative precision is added in the affine and centered coordinates. Shifts may include integral connections, nonzero prescribed data, and arbitrary real observation centers. The monotonicity persists with a fixed real linear statistic in the numerator exponent; the bound by one is not asserted for that tilted ratio.*\n\n3.  *Equality constraints are obtained by increasing their quadratic penalties to infinity. Free constants can be released when all data and real tests in question are invariant under those constants.",
          "source_id": "PM-216.3"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Allow nonnegative quadratic observation penalties and compatible linear equality constraints on a finite primary height graph.         "anchor": "stmt-sectors-single-lift",
        "exact_quote": "Let $A$ be a component of a fixed dyadic mixed geometry, with free boundary, and suppose it admits a single-valued smooth lift $v_A$ of $v$.       },

          "source_id": "PM-216.3"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $A$ be a component of a fixed dyadic mixed geometry, with free boundary, and suppose it admits a single-valued smooth lift $v_A$ of $v$.         "exact_quote": "Remove the closed disks of radius $\\rho$ about the distinct points $z_1,\\ldots,z_k$ from $D$, where $\\rho>0$ is small enough that these disks are disjoint and contained in $D$.         "anchor": "stmt-correlations-exterior-lattice",
        "exact_quote": "Suppose that $\\rho_n\\to\\rho>0$, where $\\rho$ is sufficiently small. Around $x_{i,n}$, cut out the interiors in the array division with hole shape $\\rho_nP$, using the rounding conventions of Section <a href=\"#sec:sectors\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"sec:sectors\">3</a>. Let $F_{\\rm ext}^s$ be the sector ratio in the remaining dual graph, with periods $2\\pi\\sigma_i$ about these holes. Then $$\\limsup_n\n \\left|\n \\log F_{\\rm ext}^s+\n \\frac{2\\pi k\\log(1/\\rho)+4\\pi^2H_D(\\mathbf z,\\boldsymbol\\sigma)}\n      {2a}\n \\right|\n \\leq C(\\varepsilon+\\rho).\n \\tag{13}\\label{eq:source-13}$$ The constant $C$ depends on the separated limiting configuration but not on sufficiently small $\\rho$ or $\\varepsilon$.           "anchor": "stmt-correlations-exterior-lattice",
          "exact_quote": "Suppose that $\\rho_n\\to\\rho>0$, where $\\rho$ is sufficiently small.         "anchor": "stmt-fields-lee-yang",
        "exact_quote": "Consider a finite ferromagnetic XY model with nonnegative edge couplings and all boundary angles fixed to zero.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-fields-circle-chaos",
        "exact_quote": "Let $K>1/(4\\pi)$ and let $\\Phi_D$ be the zero-Dirichlet Gaussian free field with covariance $G_D=(-\\Delta_D)^{-1}$. Where the circle of radius $\\varepsilon$ about $x$ lies in $D$, define $$V_\\varepsilon(x)=\n \\exp\\left\\{\\frac{i\\Phi_{D,\\varepsilon}(x)}{\\sqrt K}\n        +\\frac{\\mathbb E\\Phi_{D,\\varepsilon}(x)^2}{2K}\\right\\}.$$ There exists a complex random distribution $V_{K,D}\\in H^{-2}_{\\mathrm{loc}}(D)$ such that, for every $f\\in C_c^\\infty(D;\\mathbb C)$, $V_\\varepsilon(f)\\to V_{K,D}(f)$ in every finite $L^p$. For every smooth compactly supported cutoff $\\chi$, $\\chi V_\\varepsilon\\to\\chi V_{K,D}$ in $L^2(\\Omega;H^{-2})$. For $m\\geq1$, arbitrary signs $\\sigma_i\\in\\{-1,1\\}$, and $f_i\\in C_c^\\infty(D;\\mathbb C)$, $$\\label{fields:continuum-moments}\n \\mathbb E\\prod_{i=1}^m V_{K,D}^{\\sigma_i}(f_i)\n =\\int_{D^m}\\prod_{i=1}^m f_i(x_i)\n    \\exp\\left\\{-\\frac1K\\sum_{i<j}\\sigma_i\\sigma_j\n                         G_D(x_i,x_j)\\right\\}\n                  \\,dx_1\\cdots dx_m.$$ The integral is absolutely convergent. In particular, $\\mathbb EV_{K,D}(f)=\\int_D f(x)\\,dx$.",
        "source_id": "PM-216.3"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-fields-circle-chaos",
          "exact_quote": "Let $K>1/(4\\pi)$ and let $\\Phi_D$ be the zero-Dirichlet Gaussian free field with covariance $G_D=(-\\Delta_D)^{-1}$. Where the circle of radius $\\varepsilon$ about $x$ lies in $D$, define $$V_\\varepsilon(x)=\n \\exp\\left\\{\\frac{i\\Phi_{D,\\varepsilon}(x)}{\\sqrt K}\n        +\\frac{\\mathbb E\\Phi_{D,\\varepsilon}(x)^2}{2K}\\right\\}.$$ There exists a complex random distribution $V_{K,D}\\in H^{-2}_{\\mathrm{loc}}(D)$ such that, for every $f\\in C_c^\\infty(D;\\mathbb C)$, $V_\\varepsilon(f)\\to V_{K,D}(f)$ in every finite $L^p$. For every smooth compactly supported cutoff $\\chi$, $\\chi V_\\varepsilon\\to\\chi V_{K,D}$ in $L^2(\\Omega;H^{-2})$. For $m\\geq1$, arbitrary signs $\\sigma_i\\in\\{-1,1\\}$, and $f_i\\in C_c^\\infty(D;\\mathbb C)$, $$\\label{fields:continuum-moments}\n \\mathbb E\\prod_{i=1}^m V_{K,D}^{\\sigma_i}(f_i)\n =\\int_{D^m}\\prod_{i=1}^m f_i(x_i)\n    \\exp\\left\\{-\\frac1K\\sum_{i<j}\\sigma_i\\sigma_j\n                         G_D(x_i,x_j)\\right\\}\n                  \\,dx_1\\cdots dx_m.$$ The integral is absolutely convergent. In particular, $\\mathbb EV_{K,D}(f)=\\int_D f(x)\\,dx$.",

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Free-box spin mass and critical threshold** ([source](#eq:critical-parameter)): Write $$\label{eq:critical-parameter} \begin{split} C_b(r)&=\lim_{\substack{R\to\infty\\R\ge r}} \mathbb E_{R,b}\cos(\theta_0-\theta_{re_1}),\qquad e_1=(1,0),\\ m(b)&=\lim_{r\to\infty}-\frac1r\log C_b(r),\qquad b_c=\inf\{b>0:m(b)=0\}. \end{split}$$
- **Exact center magnetization** ([source](#pm-h-4)): Define $$\label{eq:center-magnetization} a_n=\mathbb E_n^0\cos\theta_0=\mathbb E_n^0e^{i\theta_0}>0.$$
- **Normalized lattice spin distribution** ([source](#eq:spin-field)): Put $K_*=2/\pi$, and define the complex random distribution $$\label{eq:spin-field} S_n=\frac1{n^2}\sum_{x\in D_n^\circ} a_n^{-1}\exp\!\left\{\frac{G_n(x,x)-G_n(0,0)}{2K_*}\right\} e^{i\theta_x}\delta_x, \qquad \delta_x(f)=f(x).$$
- **Full-variance imaginary Gaussian chaos** ([source](#pm-h-4)): For circle averages $\Phi_{D,\epsilon}$, taken where the circles lie in $D$, let $$\label{eq:target-field} V_*=\lim_{\epsilon\downarrow0} \exp\!\left\{\frac{i\Phi_{D,\epsilon}}{\sqrt{K_*}} +\frac{\mathbb E\Phi_{D,\epsilon}^2}{2K_*}\right\}.$$
- **Integral connection** ([source](#heights:weights)): An *integral connection* is an antisymmetric edge function $\alpha$ with values in $2\pi\mathbb Z$.
- **Bessel height weights** ([source](#heights:weights)): The height variables lie in $2\pi\mathbb Z$, and their edge weights are $$\label{heights:weights} p_b(j)=e^{-b}I_j(b),\qquad \prod_{e\in E}p_b\left(\frac{\nabla_eh+\alpha_e}{2\pi}\right).$$
- **Sector partition ratio** ([source](#pm-h-8)): Set $$\label{heights:sector-ratio} F_G^s=\frac{Z_G^s}{Z_G^0}.$$
- **Fixed mixed geometry** ([source](#stmt-heights-geometry)): A fixed mixed geometry is a finite union of bounded axis-parallel rectangular pieces with nondegenerate widths and faces.
- **Pin region and hard pinning** ([source](#stmt-heights-geometry)): A pin region is an aligned finite union of positive-area boxes; pinning it means setting every primary lattice height there to zero.
- **Bessel height coefficient** ([source](#pm-h-9)): The coefficient of [[C](#ref-C)] is $$a(b)=\lim_{m\to\infty} \mathop{\mathrm{Var}}\langle h,A_Gg_m\rangle.$$
- **Flat pin event** ([source](#pm-h-16)): For a nonempty vertex set $Q$, write $$[Q]=\{h_x=h_y\text{ for every }x,y\in Q\}.$$
- **Guard datum** ([source](#pm-h-20)): Its *guard datum* $u$ is the list of all height differences from that vertex to the other guard vertices, in the chosen connection gauge.
- **Regular annular interface** ([source](#pm-h-21)): Call an annulus-annulus interface *regular* when the connection is flat on their restored union and has the same period $2\pi$, $-2\pi$, or zero in both annuli.
- **Green and Robin interaction** ([source](#sec:correlations)): Define $$H_D(\mathbf z,\boldsymbol\sigma) =\sum_{i=1}^k R_D(z_i,z_i) +2\sum_{i<j}\sigma_i\sigma_jG_D(z_i,z_j).$$
- **Normalized signed point fields** ([source](#pm-h-27)): $$ The discrete maximum principle bounds $h_n(0)$ between the minimum and maximum of these boundary values, up to an error tending to zero. Shrinking $Q$ and using continuity gives Equation <a href="#eq:source-16" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:source-16">[eq:source-16]</a>. If uniform boundedness on an interior compact failed, a violating sequence would have a convergent subsequence of its locations. The convergence just proved would contradict that violation. The finitely many remaining lattice sizes cause no difficulty. ◻ </details> For $\sigma\in\{-1,1\}$, define the normalized point fields $$W_n^\sigma(x) =a_n^{-1} \exp\left\{\frac{G_n(x,x)-G_n(0,0)}{2K_*}\right\} e^{i\sigma\theta_x}.$$

|---|---|---|---|---|
| q-critical-height-coefficient | a(b_c) | = | $8\pi$ | Mass-defined critical inverse temperature. |

| q-kernel-pointwise-upper-bound | T_i^s/d_i | ≤ | $1$ | Annular, core, and joint exterior kernels. |
| q-kernel-loss-threshold | \xi | < | $1/64$ | Strictly positive average-loss tolerance. |
| q-terminal-density-bound | last-coordinate marginal density relative to the reference law | ≤ | $2$ | Eventually in the depth, for the fixed initial weight. |
| q-mean-zero-contraction-factor | \\|E_{i+1}\\|_2 | ≤ | $\frac{3\sqrt\xi}{1-2\xi}\\|E_i\\|_2$ | The fixed average-loss tolerance in the stability lemma. |
| q-long-annulus-cylinder-length | T | = | $\tfrac12\log\lambda-O(1)$ | Large annular scale ratio with the fixed clearances specified in the paper. |
| q-exterior-energy-expansion | \mathcal M | = | $2\pi k\log(1/\rho) +4\pi^2H_D(\mathbf z,\boldsymbol\sigma)+O(\rho)$ | Fixed separated configuration and sufficiently small hole radius. |
| q-nest-geometric-lower-bound | F_{\rm nest}(R_N) | ≥ | $c'\lambda^{-(N-j)/2}F_{\rm nest}(R_j)$ | Every interval of nest extension indices; the initial index denotes the filled core. |
| q-two-point-collision-upper-bound | \mathbb E_n^0\bigl[W_n^\sigma(x)W_n^\tau(y)\bigr] | ≤ | $C_E\bigl(\|x-y\|+1/n\bigr)^{-1}$ | Includes coincident lattice locations and arbitrary signs. |
| q-circle-chaos-stiffness-range | K | > | $1/(4\pi)$ | Circle Wick construction with integrable mixed kernels. |
| q-lee-yang-moment-growth | \\|W\\|_{L^p} | ≤ | $C\sqrt p\,v^{1/2}$ | All exponents in the stated moment range and arbitrary deterministic nonnegative weights. |
| q-small-square-second-moment | \mathbb E_n^0\|S_n^\sigma(1_B)\|^2 | ≤ | $C_E(d+n^{-1})^3$ | Uniform in mesh and sign, for squares in a fixed interior compact set. |

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-spin-height-duality** [claim](#stmt-heights-duality) / [proof](#proof-stmt-heights-duality): Expand interactions into nonnegative Fourier currents, route charges to the fixed boundary, and integrate the rotated closed current as a dual height. -> Spin correlations equal free dual-height sector ratios without neutrality.
- **step-critical-height-input** [claim](#stmt-heights-critical-input) / [proof](#stmt-heights-critical-input): Import the critical coefficient with the matching Bessel weights, height spacing, and free-box mass definition. -> The critical height coefficient fixes the continuum stiffness.
- **step-free-rectangle-input** [claim](#stmt-heights-free-input) / [proof](#stmt-heights-free-input): Use the cited free-rectangle Laplace and moment limits. -> Gaussian limits for free rectangular observations and mean-free smears.
- **step-mixed-height-input** [claim](#stmt-heights-mixed-input) / [proof](#stmt-heights-mixed-input): Use the cited mixed-domain limits with hard pins and finite-rank quadratic observations. -> Gaussian limits on the fixed cut and pinned geometries.
- **step-separated-pin-input** [claim](#stmt-heights-pin-input) / [proof](#stmt-heights-pin-input): Use the cited Gaussian projection determinant for positively separated absolute pins. -> A continuum determinant describing the pin interaction.
- **step-finite-affine-comparisons** [claim](#stmt-heights-finite-comparisons) / [proof](#proof-stmt-heights-finite-comparisons): Apply lattice-Gaussian precision inequalities and pass through Gaussian-chain approximations to Bessel weights on a fixed primary graph. -> Covariance and affine-ratio monotonicity, with equality constraints obtained as penalty limits.
- **step-cut-comparison** [claim](#stmt-heights-cut-comparison) / [proof](#proof-stmt-heights-cut-comparison): Represent a deleted edge by an independent increment and restore it through a matching constraint. Remove auxiliary confinement separately within each ratio. -> Cut comparison for absolute or relative data and compatible real tilts.
- **step-restoration-bound** [claim](#stmt-heights-restoration) / [proof](#proof-stmt-heights-restoration): Rearrange the prescribed-data cut comparison and use positivity of the finite Bessel sums. -> A restoration kernel is bounded by its centered zero-data value.
- **step-cell-localization** [claim](#stmt-sectors-localization) / [proof](#proof-stmt-sectors-localization): Combine cell coercivity and cutoff decay with high-mode restrictions to obtain a vanishing Hilbert–Schmidt norm and determinant interaction. -> Separated pin interactions vanish after the ordered mesh and cell limits.
- **step-single-lift-energy** [claim](#stmt-sectors-single-lift) / [proof](#proof-stmt-sectors-single-lift): Gauge away the connection, pass the confined transform to its Gaussian limit, and use cell Poincaré and weak compactness to identify the energy. -> The confined ratio recovers the lift's Dirichlet energy.
- **step-confined-multivalued-energy** [claim](#stmt-sectors-weighted) / [proof](#proof-stmt-sectors-weighted): Slit every hole using fixed strips, estimate patch and global losses through separated pins, and restore the cuts using the exact loss identity. -> The confined sector limit holds for smooth multivalued lifts.
- **step-fixed-hole-sector-limit** [claim](#stmt-sectors-positive-radius) / [proof](#proof-stmt-sectors-positive-radius): Obtain the upper bound from divergence-free real tilts and free-square limits; obtain the lower bound from confined ratios and smooth approximation to the variational infimum. -> The bare fixed-hole sector ratio converges to the exponential variational cost.
- **step-annular-flat-pin-decoupling** [claim](#stmt-annuli-flat-interaction) / [proof](#pm-h-17): Estimate the confined projection operator on a long conformal cylinder, vary confinement, and pass from absolute pins to translation-invariant flat pins. -> Flat-pin interaction becomes small across a long fixed annulus.
- **step-kernel-pointwise-and-average-bounds** [claim](#stmt-kernels-bounds) / [proof](#proof-stmt-kernels-bounds): Apply relative-data cut comparison pointwise, then impose flat end events and average over independent cut-annulus laws. -> Pointwise normalized kernel bounds and centered average lower bounds.
- **step-exact-kernel-multiplication** [claim](#stmt-kernels-multiplication) / [proof](#proof-stmt-kernels-multiplication): Parametrize block constants by differences along the block tree and condition on separating guard data. -> Exact partition-function multiplication, including a joint exterior kernel.
- **step-unit-sector-average-loss** [claim](#stmt-kernels-average-loss) / [proof](#proof-stmt-kernels-average-loss): Relate restoration averages to sector ratios, compare polygonal and circular annular energies, and combine with centered flat-pin estimates. -> Small average loss at every sufficiently large aligned regular interface.
- **step-positive-kernel-stability** [claim](#stmt-kernels-stability) / [proof](#proof-stmt-kernels-stability): Propagate constant and initial-weight densities with common normalizers, preserve positive core mass, and contract the mean-zero remainder. -> Eventual terminal density domination without an essential lower bound on the initial weight.
- **step-last-guard-domination** [claim](#stmt-kernels-last-guard) / [proof](#proof-stmt-kernels-last-guard): Identify the actual nest marginal through exact multiplication and apply positive-kernel stability separately in the centered and unit sectors. -> The last guard has the stated reference-density bound at sufficiently large depth.
- **step-continuum-exterior-energy** [claim](#stmt-correlations-exterior-energy) / [proof](#proof-stmt-correlations-exterior-energy): Rotate the gradient of the signed Dirichlet Green potential, estimate its defect from the minimizer, and compute the energy by Green's identity. -> Logarithmic hole self-energy plus Robin and pairwise Green interactions.
- **step-moving-hole-energy-control** [claim](#stmt-correlations-exterior-lattice) / [proof](#proof-stmt-correlations-exterior-lattice): Bracket moving lattice holes by fixed dyadic polygonal holes and compare their sector ratios and continuum energies. -> Exterior sector control with shape and radius errors.
- **step-exterior-attachment** [claim](#stmt-correlations-attachment) / [proof](#proof-stmt-correlations-attachment): Combine centered flat-pin bounds with the restored-to-cut sector identity and cancellation of exterior radius energies. -> Small joint attachment loss for the marked nests and the center reference.
- **step-center-normalized-correlations** [claim](#stmt-correlations-separated) / [proof](#proof-stmt-correlations-separated): Factor identical source nests, dominate propagated guard laws, and subtract the center-reference logarithm to cancel the microscopic nest contribution. -> The separated center-normalized correlation formula.
- **step-green-diagonal-correction** [claim](#stmt-correlations-green-diagonal) / [proof](#proof-stmt-correlations-green-diagonal): Subtract translated killed Green kernels, apply the discrete maximum principle on a small displacement box, and then shrink that box. -> Diagonal differences converge to Robin differences without identifying the common lattice constant.
- **step-normalized-separated-kernel** [claim](#stmt-correlations-normalized) / [proof](#proof-stmt-correlations-normalized): Insert deterministic Green normalizers into the center-normalized correlation limit and cancel Robin self-terms. -> Uniform separated convergence to the signed continuum exponential kernel.
- **step-nest-lower-bounds** [claim](#stmt-correlations-nest-bounds) / [proof](#proof-stmt-correlations-nest-bounds): Use positive center attachment and eventually bounded last-guard densities to bound extension factors, absorbing the finitely many early losses into a constant. -> Uniform center-to-nest and interscale nest lower bounds.
- **step-two-point-collision-bound** [claim](#stmt-correlations-diagonal) / [proof](#proof-stmt-correlations-diagonal): Cut disjoint source nests at a distance-dependent scale, use nest lower bounds, and handle microscopic separation by positivity of the fixed core. -> An integrable bound through the two-point diagonal and positivity for all signed products.
- **step-lee-yang-moments** [claim](#stmt-fields-lee-yang) / [proof](#proof-stmt-fields-lee-yang): Introduce a uniform common rotation, use the Lee–Yang product representation to bound real projections, and integrate the resulting tail bound. -> Absolute moments controlled by the second moment and positivity of signed products.
- **step-continuum-circle-chaos** [claim](#stmt-fields-circle-chaos) / [proof](#proof-stmt-fields-circle-chaos): Bound unequal-radius circle covariances, dominate mixed kernels using covariance positivity and nearest-neighbor distances, and construct compatible localized Sobolev limits. -> The continuum random distribution and its absolutely convergent mixed moments.
- **step-smeared-field-bounds** [claim](#stmt-fields-moment-bounds) / [proof](#proof-stmt-fields-moment-bounds): Sum the two-point collision bound over lattice shells, apply Lee–Yang to nonnegative weights, and decompose complex tests into nonnegative parts. -> Uniform smeared moments and small-square estimates.
- **step-all-mixed-moments** [claim](#stmt-fields-moments) / [proof](#proof-stmt-fields-moments): Remove pair-collision neighborhoods using positivity and box moment bounds, then apply separated convergence and Riemann summation off the diagonals. -> Convergence of every signed smeared mixed moment.
- **step-joint-test-laws** [claim](#stmt-fields-law) / [proof](#proof-stmt-fields-law): Use exponential integrability to establish moment determinacy of real projections and identify joint complex laws by the Cramér–Wold criterion. -> Full-sequence joint convergence of smeared evaluations and their mixed moments.
- **step-local-sobolev-limit** [claim](#stmt-fields-tightness) / [proof](#proof-stmt-fields-tightness): Bound localized Fourier coefficients, use compact Sobolev embedding and a cutoff exhaustion, and identify subsequential laws by their test evaluations. -> Tightness and full-sequence convergence in the local Sobolev topology.
- **step-main-field-theorem** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-fields-tightness): Combine mixed moment convergence, joint test-function laws, and local Sobolev convergence with the exact normalization fixed at the outset. -> The critical spin-field theorem with the same exact center normalization at every order.

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **invariant-critical-field-normalization**: The full-sequence field limit uses the actual center magnetization and the same lattice Green correction for every test function and correlation order. The limiting imaginary exponential uses the entire Dirichlet variance.
- **invariant-compatible-edge-restoration**: An edge-restoration factor is positive and bounded by its centered zero-data value. Free component constants remain quotiented separately before restoration, and relative integer translations are summed when components are joined.
- **invariant-positive-kernel-stability**: Positive kernels with sufficiently small average loss eventually bound the terminal marginal density by the stated universal bound, even when the fixed positive initial weight has no positive essential lower bound.
- **invariant-full-variance-circle-chaos**: Circle regularizations with the full variance counterterm converge to a random distribution. Its mixed moments retain arbitrary signs and are given by absolutely convergent Green-kernel integrals.
- **invariant-ferromagnetic-moment-control**: For nonnegative deterministic spin weights, the absolute moment bound depends on the second moment. Signed spin correlations remain nonnegative even with repeated vertices.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-use-inferred-magnetization-asymptotics**: Replace the exact center magnetization by an assumed power law or logarithmic correction and claim the same normalized limit. -> The microscopic amplitude cancellation is tied to the actual observable; an unproved replacement can alter the limiting normalization.
- **anti-use-free-boundary-transfer**: Apply the fixed-boundary field theorem directly to the free-boundary infinite-volume correlation. -> The change of boundary law requires amplitude and boundary-to-bulk information beyond the field limit.
- **anti-use-shrinking-geometry-height-limit**: Invoke a fixed-geometry height or pin limit uniformly while holes shrink to the lattice scale. -> The cited inputs do not give uniform conditional limits over microscopic pin data or arbitrary varying geometry.
- **anti-use-fixed-relative-constants**: Fix the relative height translation of cut blocks when restoring their interface. -> The resulting partition sum omits translation copies and invalidates the exact gluing factorization.
- **anti-use-uniform-core-forgetting-time**: Treat the eventual last-guard density bound as a uniform finite-depth guarantee over arbitrary positive microscopic cores. -> The proof chooses a positive mass threshold from the fixed initial weight; positivity alone supplies no uniform onset over all such weights.
- **anti-use-separated-correlations-at-collisions**: Pass directly from separated-point correlation convergence to all smeared mixed moments. -> Separated convergence leaves collision regions and repeated lattice sites uncontrolled.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `not_applicable`. The principal result is convergence in law and mixed moments of random distributions under lattice refinement, rather than a finite constructive computation. The source states: "They converge there, along the full sequence, to the law of $V_*$.” Its positive-kernel bridge is also eventual: "No positive essential lower bound on $g$ is required." The proof obtains the onset from the fixed initial weight and supplies no effective uniform depth. Finite sample calculations would therefore check examples without deciding the asserted field limit or its quantified hypotheses.

Set $\\theta_x=0$ on $D_n\\setminus D_n^\\circ$, and integrate only the interior angles in the probability law $$\\label{eq:pinned-spin-law}\n \\frac1{Z_n^0}\n \\exp\\!\\left\\{b_c\\sum_{\\{x,y\\}\\in E_n}\n                         \\cos(\\theta_x-\\theta_y)\\right\\}\n \\prod_{x\\in D_n^\\circ}\\frac{d\\theta_x}{2\\pi}.$$ Its expectation is denoted by $\\mathbb E_n^0$. In particular edges incident to the fixed boundary are retained. Define $$\\label{eq:center-magnetization}\n a_n=\\mathbb E_n^0\\cos\\theta_0=\\mathbb E_n^0e^{i\\theta_0}>0.$$ Angle reflection proves the equality, and the positive Fourier-current expansion proves strict positivity. Thus $a_n$ is a specified observable of <a href=\"#eq:pinned-spin-law\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:pinned-spin-law\">[eq:pinned-spin-law]</a>, not a sequence chosen to fit a limit.",

Put $K_*=2/\\pi$, and define the complex random distribution $$\\label{eq:spin-field}\n S_n=\\frac1{n^2}\\sum_{x\\in D_n^\\circ}\n a_n^{-1}\\exp\\!\\left\\{\\frac{G_n(x,x)-G_n(0,0)}{2K_*}\\right\\}\n e^{i\\theta_x}\\delta_x,\n \\qquad \\delta_x(f)=f(x).$$ The center factor and the Green correction in this definition are the same for every test function and every correlation order.",

This is the full-variance imaginary Gaussian chaos: its counterterm uses the entire variance, including its finite spatial part. In this convention $$\\mathbb EV_*(f)=\\int_D f(z)\\,dz,\\qquad\n \\mathbb E\\bigl[V_*(z)\\overline{V_*(w)}\\bigr]\n       =\\exp\\{G_D(z,w)/K_*\\},$$ where the second expression denotes the off-diagonal moment kernel. Its singularity has exponent $1/4$. Existence and the precise mixed-moment interpretation are recalled in Lemma <a href=\"#fields:circle-chaos\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"fields:circle-chaos\">30</a>; see also [[JSW2020](#ref-JSW2020), [B](#ref-B)].",

            "source_object": "stmt-kernels-stability"

          {

            "exact_quote": "Let $K>1/(4\\pi)$ and let $\\Phi_D$ be the zero-Dirichlet Gaussian free field with covariance $G_D=(-\\Delta_D)^{-1}$.",
            "source_object": "stmt-fields-circle-chaos"
          },
          {
            "anchor": "stmt-fields-circle-chaos",
            "exact_quote": "Where the circle of radius $\\varepsilon$ about $x$ lies in $D$, define $$V_\\varepsilon(x)=\n \\exp\\left\\{\\frac{i\\Phi_{D,\\varepsilon}(x)}{\\sqrt K}\n        +\\frac{\\mathbb E\\Phi_{D,\\varepsilon}(x)^2}{2K}\\right\\}.$$",
            "source_object": "stmt-fields-circle-chaos"
          }
        ],
        "id": "invariant-full-variance-circle-chaos",
        "quantifiers": [
          {
            "anchor": "stmt-fields-circle-chaos",
            "exact_quote": "There exists a complex random distribution $V_{K,D}\\in H^{-2}_{\\mathrm{loc}}(D)$ such that, for every $f\\in C_c^\\infty(D;\\mathbb C)$, $V_\\varepsilon(f)\\to V_{K,D}(f)$ in every finite $L^p$.",
            "source_object": "stmt-fields-circle-chaos"
          },
          {
            "anchor": "stmt-fields-circle-chaos",
            "exact_quote": "For $m\\geq1$, arbitrary signs $\\sigma_i\\in\\{-1,1\\}$, and $f_i\\in C_c^\\infty(D;\\mathbb C)$, $$\\label{fields:continuum-moments}\n \\mathbb E\\prod_{i=1}^m V_{K,D}^{\\sigma_i}(f_i)\n =\\int_{D^m}\\prod_{i=1}^m f_i(x_i)\n    \\exp\\left\\{-\\frac1K\\sum_{i<j}\\sigma_i\\sigma_j\n                         G_D(x_i,x_j)\\right\\}\n                  \\,dx_1\\cdots dx_m.$$",
            "source_object": "stmt-fields-circle-chaos"
          }
        ],
        "regime": "Zero-Dirichlet Gaussian free fields in the stated stiffness range, with smooth compactly supported tests and circles contained in the domain.",

            "source_object": "stmt-fields-lee-yang"

<a id="pm-s5"></a>
## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The field convergence theorem supplies a mathematical specification for a particular spin model, but no software implementation, executable approximation, or error-controlled computational procedure. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The continuum field describes an equilibrium scaling limit. The paper does not construct a finite sampling procedure or a time evolution suitable for simulation. |
| tools | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The finite comparison rules can guide mathematical checks on partition ratios, but evaluating their infinite height sums and certifying computational truncation are not supplied as a tool construction. |
| algorithms | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Exact multiplication expresses a partition function through conditional kernels and integrals. It does not give a finite algorithm for evaluating those kernels or integrating their height-difference data. |
| data_structures | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Guard data are mathematical lists of height differences used for conditioning. The paper gives no storage representation, update procedure, or finite encoding contract for their unbounded values. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The lattice and Green-function formulas define mathematical operators without specifying a hardware mapping, bounded arithmetic, or an accelerated evaluation construction. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Magnetic sectors and integral gauge changes describe equivalent height partition sums. They provide no cryptographic primitive, adversarial model, or security reduction. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Gaussian limits for specified Bessel height observations do not construct a learning rule, parameter estimator, or trainable model. Their applicability depends on fixed geometry and the prescribed observation regime. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The sector theorem characterizes a continuum variational infimum, but supplies no finite optimization method, discretization error bound, or stopping certificate for computing it. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Positive-kernel stability concerns probability measures propagated through abstract kernels. It supplies no distributed protocol, communication model, or fault-handling construction. |
| other | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper directly contributes to mathematical physics and probability through a continuum random-distribution construction. That analytical construction does not itself provide an additional finite engineering contract. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-fixed-boundary-critical-law** (hypothesis): The spin-field limit concerns the specified critical square with boundary angles fixed to zero and boundary-incident edges retained. Blocked use: ['Apply the field limit to a different boundary law or a different definition of criticality without a separate comparison.']
- **limit-exact-center-normalization** (formal_scope): The normalizing scalar is the actual center magnetization. The field theorem does not justify replacing it by a fitted amplitude, power law, or logarithmic correction. Blocked use: ['Substitute an assumed asymptotic normalizer and assert that the limiting field is unchanged.']
- **limit-unscaled-green-operator** (hypothesis): The deterministic correction uses the unscaled lattice Laplacian and its inverse; changing this convention changes the normalization. Blocked use: ['Insert a continuum-scaled lattice inverse into the stated field definition without adjusting its factors.']
- **limit-full-variance-counterterm** (formal_scope): The target uses the entire Dirichlet variance, including its finite spatial part. Blocked use: ['Remove only the divergent part of the variance and identify the resulting field with the stated target without a spatial correction.']
- **limit-companion-critical-input** (hypothesis): The critical height coefficient is an independent cited input with matching weight, height-spacing, edge, and mass-threshold conventions. Blocked use: ['Present the spin-field argument as an independent proof of the critical height coefficient.', 'Continue a low-temperature spin theorem to criticality without the critical inputs.']
- **limit-fixed-mixed-geometry** (hypothesis): Mixed-domain height limits require nondegenerate rectangular pieces, polygonal Lipschitz components, and positive-area aligned pin regions. Point contacts do not provide admissible connections. Blocked use: ['Apply the mixed-domain input to vanishing-width passages, point-connected pieces, or microscopic pin sets.']
- **limit-free-component-tests** (hypothesis): A smear on a released free component must have an exactly centered discrete profile; continuum mean-zero sampling alone does not establish that condition. Blocked use: ['Test a quotient height law with an uncentered discrete density.']
- **limit-separated-pins-and-ordered-limits** (approximation): Geometry, observation boxes, and positive pin separation are fixed before lattice refinement. Subsequent geometric limits cannot be interchanged with that mesh limit using the cited inputs alone. Blocked use: ['Invoke the pin determinant uniformly for shrinking pin separation or arbitrary microscopic prescribed data.']
- **limit-component-local-cut-data** (hypothesis): Cut comparison requires matching retained observation penalties, compatible prescribed data, and component-local observations and relative-data sets after cutting. Real tests must annihilate released constants. Blocked use: ['Compare cut and restored ratios after retaining an observation that couples newly disconnected components.', 'Release a component constant while retaining a real test that detects it.']
- **limit-tilted-ratio-upper-bound** (formal_scope): Real numerator tilts retain precision monotonicity, but the untilted upper bound is not asserted for the tilted ratio. Blocked use: ['Bound a tilted affine partition ratio by its centered counterpart solely from the untilted comparison.']
- **limit-relative-translations-and-block-adjacencies** (hypothesis): Exact gluing sums relative integer translations and uses the specified block adjacencies and full separating guard data. Several nests share a joint exterior kernel. Blocked use: ['Fix relative block constants during restoration.', 'Multiply independent exterior attachment factors without proving their independence.', 'Apply the annular multiplication formula with additional block adjacencies.']
- **limit-regular-unit-sector-average-loss** (hypothesis): The regular-interface average-loss estimate concerns flat restored connections in the centered and unit-period sectors. Its parameter choices and aligned scale threshold precede its use along a nest. Blocked use: ['Apply the same average-loss guarantee to interfaces introducing magnetic sources or to arbitrary periods without further estimates.']
- **limit-fixed-positive-core-eventuality** (nonconstructive): Terminal density domination is eventual for a fixed almost-everywhere positive initial weight. The theorem supplies no uniform onset over arbitrary positive cores. Blocked use: ['Treat the eventual density bound as an immediate guarantee at every nest depth.', 'Allow the initial core weight to vary with the depth and retain the conclusion without additional control.']
- **limit-separated-kernel-versus-collisions** (approximation): Uniform separated correlation convergence requires a fixed positive lower bound on mutual distances. Passing to smeared moments also requires the collision bound, positivity, and absolute moment estimates. Blocked use: ['Use the separated kernel directly at repeated lattice locations.', 'Infer convergence of all moment sums from separated convergence alone.']
- **limit-ferromagnetic-moment-hypotheses** (hypothesis): The direct Lee–Yang absolute moment estimate assumes nonnegative edge couplings, fixed zero boundary angles, and nonnegative deterministic observable weights. Complex weights are treated by decomposition afterward. Blocked use: ['Apply the direct weighted inequality to frustrated couplings.', 'Use arbitrary complex weights in the direct nonnegative-weight statement without the decomposition argument.']
- **limit-circle-chaos-stiffness-and-locality** (hypothesis): The circle-field construction requires the stated strict stiffness range, circles contained in the domain, and compactly supported smooth tests. It establishes a random distribution rather than pointwise limiting spin values. Blocked use: ['Extend the circle-chaos assertion to the threshold or beyond its stated stiffness range.', 'Evaluate the limiting field pointwise using the distributional convergence theorem.', 'Use boundary-crossing averaging circles without another construction.']
- **limit-free-boundary-amplitudes** (formal_scope): The fixed-boundary spin-field theorem neither determines center-magnetization asymptotics nor transfers by itself to free-boundary infinite-volume correlations. Blocked use: ['Deduce a center-magnetization asymptotic formula from this theorem alone.', 'Assert the free-boundary infinite-volume correlation asymptotic without additional amplitude and boundary-to-bulk information.']
- **limit-no-finite-computational-certificate** (nonconstructive): The continuum field limit is obtained through ordered limits, compactness, and identification of subsequential laws. A finite computational approximation guarantee is ABSENT. Blocked use: ['Treat agreement on finite lattices as a certificate of the full-sequence random-distribution theorem.', 'Assign a certified numerical stopping rule from the qualitative convergence result.']

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


Using the companion critical Bessel-height and pin-limit theorems, we prove that at the mass-defined critical inverse temperature of the nearest-neighbor XY model on the square lattice, the spin field on a square with boundary angles fixed to zero converges along the full sequence to the full-variance imaginary exponential of a zero-Dirichlet Gaussian free field with stiffness $`2/\pi`$. The normalization uses the exact center magnetization and the lattice Green-function factor. Convergence holds in law in $`H^{-3}_{\mathrm{loc}}((-1,1)^2)`$; all mixed moments and joint laws of fields smeared against smooth compactly supported test functions in $`(-1,1)^2`$ converge as well.

| [stmt-correlations-diagonal](#stmt-correlations-diagonal) | proposition | `correlations:diagonal` | 28 | For every compact $E\Subset D$, there is $C_E<\infty$ such that, for all $n\geq1$, all $x,y\in E\cap D_n^\circ$, including $x=y$, and all $\sigma,\tau\in\{-1,1\}$, |
| [stmt-fields-lee-yang](#stmt-fields-lee-yang) | lemma | `fields:lee-yang` | 29 | Consider a finite ferromagnetic XY model with nonnegative edge couplings and all boundary angles fixed to zero. |
| [stmt-fields-circle-chaos](#stmt-fields-circle-chaos) | lemma | `fields:circle-chaos` | 30 | Let $K>1/(4\pi)$ and let $\Phi_D$ be the zero-Dirichlet Gaussian free field with covariance $G_D=(-\Delta_D)^{-1}$. |
| [stmt-fields-moment-bounds](#stmt-fields-moment-bounds) | lemma | `fields:moment-bounds` | 31 | For every compact $E\Subset D$, every bounded complex function $f$ supported in $E$, and every $p\geq2$, |
| [stmt-fields-moments](#stmt-fields-moments) | proposition | `fields:moments` | 32 | For every $m\geq1$, signs $\sigma_i\in\{-1,1\}$, and $f_i\in C_c^\infty(D;\mathbb C)$, |
